  • REST API — every endpoint, parameter and status code.
  • gRPC API — services, streaming shapes and metadata.
  • Authentication — token lifecycle behind the login step.
  • Encryption at Rest — what the encrypted chunk flag means.
  • Configuration reference — chunking, caching and limits used above.

REST API

The REST API is the primary HTTP interface of file-storage, served by axum on server.port (default 8080) under the base path /api/v1. Every request and response uses JSON (except binary uploads/downloads), and every failure returns the same {"error":{"code","message"}} envelope. This page documents every endpoint, its authentication requirement, request/response schemas, and every error it can return.

Overview

StatusMeaningTypical error.code
200OK — login, refresh, list, meta, admin results—
201Created — upload, tenant creation—
204No Content — delete, logout, internal purge—
206Partial Content — range download—
304Not Modified — If-None-Match matched—
400Bad request — bad JSON, UUID, Range or hashvalidation_failed
401Missing/invalid Authorization or X-Api-Keyunauthorized
403Role or tenant scope violationforbidden
404Unknown file or tenantnot_found
409Conflicting state — e.g. resync with replication disabledconflict
413Body exceeds limits.max_body / limits.max_file_sizepayload_too_large
416Range unsatisfiable (Content-Range: bytes */size)range_not_satisfiable
500Server-side failure (corruption, IO, database, …)hash_mismatch, corruption, io_error, database_error, …
502Replication target failedreplication_error
503Not ready (probe) or degraded dependencyservice_unavailable

How it works

Requests flow through middleware in order: request-id assignment → body size cap (DefaultBodyLimit = limits.max_body) → rate limiting → auth. When rate_limit.enabled is true, a tower_governor token bucket keyed by client IP guards all of /api/v1 (rate_limit.per_second / rate_limit.burst); over-limit callers receive 429 Too Many Requests (not part of the StorageError catalog — it is emitted by the governor layer). Requests behind a reverse proxy are keyed by proxy IP; see Troubleshooting.

Authentication is Authorization: Bearer <jwt> for /api/v1 (except /auth/*, which is public) and X-Api-Key for /internal/*. /health, /ready and /metrics are public. Successful uploads are content-defined chunked, hashed (BLAKE3), compressed and optionally encrypted; uploading a file under a name that already exists in your tenant replaces the previous file — the old manifest is soft-deleted and stays visible only with include_deleted=true (admin). Deletes are soft by default and purged by GC after retention.grace_period_secs.

Usage

POST /api/v1/auth/login — public

Body {"username":"admin","password":"admin123"} → 200 {"access_token","refresh_token","token_type":"Bearer","expires_in":900}. Errors: unauthorized (401), validation_failed (400).

POST /api/v1/auth/refresh — public

Body {"refresh_token":"..."} → 200 same shape; the presented token is revoked (single-use rotation). Errors: 401, 400.

POST /api/v1/auth/logout — public

Body {"refresh_token":"..."} → 204, idempotent. Revokes the refresh token; the access token stays valid until exp (stateless).

POST /api/v1/files — Bearer, write role

Two accepted forms:

  • multipart (multipart/form-data): required file field plus optional name, tags (comma-separated), mime/content_type text fields (each ≤ 4096 bytes). The file field is spooled to storage.temp_dir.
  • raw body: any content type; metadata from query string ?name=&mime=&tags=a,b. Content-Type becomes the MIME when mime is unset.

Returns 201:

{"file_id":"01930000-0000-7000-8000-0000000000aa","name":"report.pdf","mime":"application/pdf",
 "size":1048576,"size_stored":524288,"chunk_count":8,"content_hash":"<64-hex blake3>",
 "dedup_chunks":3,"dedup_bytes":393216,"created_at_ms":1730000000000}

Size is checked early against Content-Length and again while streaming (limits.max_file_size, default 10 GiB). Errors: 400 (bad multipart, missing file), 401, 403 (viewer role), 413.

GET /api/v1/files — Bearer

Query paramTypeDefaultNotes
limitint50clamped to 1..1000
offsetint0pagination skip
tagstring—takes precedence over name; exact match
namestring—exact match
include_deletedboolfalseadmin only

→ 200 {"total","limit","offset","items":[{file_id,name,mime,size,size_stored,chunk_count,tags,created_at_ms,deleted}]}. Errors: 401, 403 (include_deleted without admin).

GET /api/v1/files/:id — Bearer

Downloads the full file. Headers: ETag: "<64-hex>", Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600, Content-Disposition: inline; filename="…", X-Content-Hash. If-None-Match matching the ETag → 304 (no body). Errors: 400 (bad UUID), 401, 403 (cross-tenant without admin), 404 — soft-deleted files read as 404.

GET /api/v1/files/:id/range — Bearer

Range header required: bytes=start-end (inclusive end), bytes=-N (suffix), open end bytes=10-. Multi-range requests take the first range only. End is clamped to the file size. → 206 with Content-Range: bytes start-end/size; unsatisfiable start → 416 with Content-Range: bytes */size. Missing/invalid header → 400. Also 401/403/404.

GET /api/v1/files/:id/meta?chunks=true|false — Bearer

→ 200 FileMetaDto: file_id,name,tenant_id,mime,size,size_stored,content_hash,chunk_count,tags,created_at_ms,updated_at_ms,deleted,version,href, plus optional chunks:[{offset,size_original,size_stored,hash,compressed,encrypted}] (first 1000 chunks; chunks_truncated=true if more). Errors: 400/401/403/404.

DELETE /api/v1/files/:id?purge=true|false — Bearer

Soft delete (default) → 204; sets deleted_at_ms, keeps blobs until GC. purge=true is admin only: removes the manifest, decrements chunk refcounts and deletes blobs reaching zero. Errors: 400, 401, 403 (purge without admin; any delete without write role), 404.

POST /api/v1/tenants, GET /api/v1/tenants — Bearer, admin

Body {"name":"acme"} → 201 TenantDto {id,name,created_at_ms,file_count,bytes_used}; GET → array. Errors: 401, 403.

GET /api/v1/audit?limit= — Bearer, admin

Most recent events first, limit clamped 1..1000 (default 100) → array of {id,ts_ms,actor,tenant_id,action,object,ip,status,detail}. Errors: 401, 403.

POST /api/v1/admin/gc — Bearer, admin

Runs one GC pass → 200 {manifests_purged,chunks_removed,orphans_removed,bytes_reclaimed}. Errors: 401, 403, 500.

POST /api/v1/admin/resync/:id — Bearer, admin

Re-pushes a file (manifest + chunk blobs) to the replication target → 200 {file_id,replicated:true}. Errors: 401, 403, 404, 409 when replication is disabled / no target configured, 502.

Probes — public

  • GET /health → 200 {status,version,uptime_secs}.
  • GET /ready → 200 {status:"ready",database:true,storage:true} or 503 with status:"degraded" and failing booleans.
  • GET /metrics → 200 Prometheus text (see Monitoring).

/internal/* — X-Api-Key

Peer replication endpoints, guarded by a constant-time comparison against replication.api_key (not JWT): POST /internal/chunk?hash=&plain_hash=&size_original=&size_stored=&compressed=&encrypted= (raw blob body), GET /internal/chunk/:hash (raw blob), POST /internal/manifest (msgpack manifest), DELETE /internal/manifest/:id, POST /internal/batch (chunk frame batch), GET /internal/file/:id (bundle), POST /internal/file/:id (apply bundle). Internal transfers have a 2 GiB ceiling; writes are idempotent. Errors: 401 (missing/invalid key), 400 (hash must be 64 hex chars), 404, 500.

Examples

TOKEN=$(curl -s localhost:8080/api/v1/auth/login \
  -d '{"username":"alice","password":"alice123"}' | jq -r .access_token)

curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -F file=@report.pdf -F name=report.pdf -F tags=reports,q3 \
  localhost:8080/api/v1/files

curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "Range: bytes=0-1023" \
  -o part.bin localhost:8080/api/v1/files/<file_id>/range

A 400 response looks like:

{"error":{"code":"validation_failed","message":"unsupported Range header 'bytes=x-y'"}}

gRPC API

The gRPC API is served by tonic on a second listener, server.grpc_port (default 9090), sharing the same state as the REST API. All services live in the protobuf package file_storage.v1 (protos under proto/file_storage/v1/), so you can generate clients in any language or use the bundled Rust SDK. This page catalogs every service and RPC, streaming semantics, and how errors map onto gRPC status codes.

Overview

ServicePurposeAuth
file_storage.v1.FileServiceUpload, Download, GetManifest, ListFiles, DeleteFileBearer JWT
file_storage.v1.AuthServiceLogin, Refresh, Logoutpublic
file_storage.v1.AdminServiceTenants, TriggerGC, Resync, QueryAuditadmin JWT
file_storage.v1.ReplicationServiceInternal peer protocol (ReplicateBatch, PullChunk, PullManifest)x-api-key metadata
grpc.health.v1.HealthCheck/Watch for the four services + overall ""public

grpc.reflection.v1.ServerReflection is also enabled (project protos + health descriptors), which is why plain grpcurl works against the server. The full RPC surface is summarized below; the messages are defined in the repository’s proto/file_storage/v1/*.proto — hashes (hash, plain_hash, original_hash) are raw 32-byte BLAKE3.

How it works

Metadata conventions: send authorization: Bearer <jwt> for user RPCs, x-api-key for ReplicationService, and optionally x-request-id (propagated when present, otherwise a UUIDv7 is minted). Two honest caveats about the implementation:

  • tonic interceptors run for unary RPCs only. Unary calls get JWT verification, the admin gate, the per-tenant rate limit and request-id propagation via interceptors; streaming handlers (Upload, Download, ReplicateBatch, PullChunk) verify the token / api-key in-handler. Semantics are identical.
  • Rate limiting: unary RPCs pass a per-tenant token bucket (rate_limit.per_second/burst; disabled → passthrough) and fail with RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED. Streaming RPCs are not rate limited — their protection is limits.max_file_size and the expected_size guard.

Server tuning: max message 256 MiB, HTTP/2 keepalive 30 s / timeout 20 s, 64 concurrent streams per connection, stream window 4 MiB / connection window 8 MiB, TCP keepalive 30 s, gzip compression supported both directions. TLS/mTLS for the listener is configured under server.grpc_tls (see TLS). Panics in handlers are recovered and surface as gRPC status 13 (INTERNAL).

Usage

Every RPC maps internal StorageError variants onto gRPC codes per the Errors table — e.g. a missing file is NOT_FOUND, a viewer attempting a write is PERMISSION_DENIED, an oversized stream is RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED.

FileService

RPCTypeRequestResponseNotable statuses
Uploadclient-streamingUploadChunk stream (first: header{name,mime_type,expected_size,tenant_id,tags}, then data)UploadResponse{manifest,deduped_bytes,stored_bytes,dedup_chunks,compression_ratio}INVALID_ARGUMENT (empty/bad name, header not first), RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED (size cap), UNAUTHENTICATED, PERMISSION_DENIED
Downloadserver-streamingDownloadRequest{file_id,start?,end?} — half-open exclusive end, clamped to sizeDownloadChunk stream (first: header{file_id,size,total_size,mime_type,original_hash,start,end}, then data)NOT_FOUND, OUT_OF_RANGE (start ≥ size), DATA_LOSS (hash mismatch)
GetManifestunaryGetManifestRequest{file_id,include_chunks}ManifestNOT_FOUND, INVALID_ARGUMENT (bad UUID)
ListFilesunaryListFilesRequest{limit,offset,tag,name,include_deleted} — limit clamped 1..1000 default 50; tag exact / name substring; include_deleted silently ignored for non-adminsListFilesResponse{files, total}UNAUTHENTICATED
DeleteFileunaryDeleteFileRequest{file_id,purge}DeleteFileResponse{deleted_at,purged}NOT_FOUND, PERMISSION_DENIED (purge or write without role)

AuthService

RPCTypeRequestResponseStatuses
LoginunaryLoginRequest{username,password}LoginResponse{access_token,refresh_token,token_type,expires_in,expires_at}UNAUTHENTICATED (bad credentials), INVALID_ARGUMENT
RefreshunaryRefreshRequest{refresh_token}LoginResponse (old token revoked)UNAUTHENTICATED (unknown/expired)
LogoutunaryLogoutRequest{token}Empty — idempotent—

AdminService (all admin-only)

RPCTypeRequest → ResponseStatuses
CreateTenantunary{name} (1..=128 chars) → TenantINVALID_ARGUMENT, PERMISSION_DENIED
GetTenantunary{id} → Tenant (quota_bytes reserved, always 0)NOT_FOUND, INVALID_ARGUMENT
ListTenantsunary{limit} → {tenants,total}PERMISSION_DENIED
TriggerGCunary{mode: MODE_UNSPECIFIED|REFCOUNT|REACHABILITY, dry_run} → GCResultdry_run=true → UNIMPLEMENTED (“gc dry_run is not supported yet; run without dry_run”); PERMISSION_DENIED
Resyncunary{file_id} → {file_id,replicated}NOT_FOUND, ALREADY_EXISTS (replication disabled), UNAVAILABLE

ReplicationService (x-api-key)

RPCTypeRequest → ResponseStatuses
ReplicateBatchbidistream of ReplicationOp{put_chunk|put_manifest|delete_file|flush} → stream of ReplicationAck{ops_acked,success,error}UNAUTHENTICATED (bad key), INTERNAL
PullChunkserver-streaming{hash (32 B)} → PullChunkResponse{header|data} — ≤ 64 KiB pieces, raw stored blobNOT_FOUND, UNAUTHENTICATED
PullManifestunary{file_id} → ManifestBundle{manifest}NOT_FOUND, UNAUTHENTICATED

All ops are idempotent, so replaying a batch after a crash is safe. Health probes run in the background every 10 s (redb ping + storage-root writability) and flip NOT_SERVING within ≤ 15 s of failure.

Streaming semantics

  • Header-first framing. Upload starts with exactly one UploadHeader; Download starts with exactly one DownloadHeader. Everything after is data.
  • The client does NOT chunk content. Send raw bytes in ~1 MiB pieces (the Rust SDK does this); the server performs FastCDC chunking, hashing, compression and encryption internally.
  • expected_size guard. When set and the stream exceeds it, the upload is rejected with RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED and the server stops buffering.
  • Backpressure is HTTP/2 flow control end-to-end. The download path drains retrieval through a bounded mpsc channel (depth 32 × 256 KiB pieces); the upload SDK bridges a bounded 32-message channel. Honor gRPC READY signals; never buffer unbounded.
  • Abort semantics: canceling the stream (dropping the request/response future) tears down the pump; server-side tasks observe a send failure and stop, discarding partial state. Aborted uploads store nothing.
  • REST vs gRPC ranges differ: REST Range is inclusive-end (bytes=10-19), gRPC end is exclusive (slice semantics).

REST or gRPC?

OperationPrefer REST whenPrefer gRPC when
Login/refreshscripts, curlSDK-managed token lifecycle
Uploadone-shot tools, browsers (multipart)large files, backpressure-sensitive pipelines
DownloadHTTP caching (ETag/304), CDN-style clientsstreaming into disk/network with flow control, ranged reads without header parsing
Metadata/list/deleteadmin scriptslow-latency typed access from Go/Rust/Python services
GC/resync/audithumans + curlautomation in services

Examples

Rust SDK (file_storage::grpc::FileStorageClient — 1 MiB upload pieces, retry policy 3 attempts 100 ms→2 s on Unavailable/DeadlineExceeded/Aborted):

use file_storage::grpc::FileStorageClient;

#[tokio::main]
async fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let mut c = FileStorageClient::connect("http://127.0.0.1:9090").await?;
    c.login("alice", "alice123").await?; // token stored on the client

    let header = file_storage::grpc::UploadHeader {
        name: "notes.txt".into(),
        mime_type: "text/plain".into(),
        tags: vec!["notes".into()],
        ..Default::default()
    };
    let reader = tokio::fs::File::open("notes.txt").await?;
    let resp = c.upload(header, reader).await?;
    println!("stored {} B, dedup {} B", resp.stored_bytes, resp.deduped_bytes);
    Ok(())
}

grpcurl (reflection is enabled):

grpcurl -plaintext -d '{"username":"alice","password":"alice123"}' \
  127.0.0.1:9090 file_storage.v1.AuthService/Login

grpcurl -plaintext -H "authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -d '{"file_id":"01930000-0000-7000-8000-0000000000aa"}' \
  127.0.0.1:9090 file_storage.v1.FileService/GetManifest

Errors

Every failure across both protocols comes from one unified error type, so a given condition always surfaces with the same semantics whether you called REST or gRPC. REST encodes it as an HTTP status plus a stable snake_case code in a JSON envelope; gRPC encodes it as a tonic status code plus the error message. This page is the complete mapping table plus debugging hints for the failures you will actually hit.

Overview

The REST error body always has this shape (error.code is one of the stable strings in the table below, error.message is human-readable):

{"error":{"code":"not_found","message":"not found: file 0193…-aa"}}

The gRPC equivalent is Status{code: NOT_FOUND, message: "not found: file 0193…-aa"} — the same Display text is used as the status message.

How it works

Handlers return Result<T, StorageError>; the framework (REST IntoResponse, gRPC status_from_error) maps the variant to a transport code. 5xx outcomes are logged at error level with their code, 4xx at warn. Rate-limit rejections are the exception: on REST the tower_governor layer answers 429 Too Many Requests directly (plain text, not the JSON envelope); on gRPC the per-tenant bucket fails unary calls with RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED.

Usage

Full error catalog

Serialization500INTERNALserialization_error

Unavailable503UNAVAILABLEservice_unavailable
Internal500INTERNALinternal_error

Examples

Common scenarios and debugging hints

  • File not found (404 / NOT_FOUND) — Files are tenant-scoped: a valid UUID from another tenant reads as 404 unless you are admin with cross-tenant access. Soft-deleted files also read as 404 on normal reads; list with include_deleted=true (admin) to see them, or check GET /files/:id/meta after a same-name overwrite (the old file is soft-deleted).
  • Quota errors (409/413 “quota”) — There is no quota enforcement: Tenant.quota_bytes is reserved and always reported as 0 (= unlimited). The only size caps are limits.max_file_size (per upload, default 10 GiB) and limits.max_body (HTTP body, default 1 GiB). If you see 413, compare your payload against those two values.
  • Rate limited — REST: HTTP 429 from the governor when rate_limit.enabled=true, keyed by client IP (rate_limit.per_second/burst). Behind a proxy, every client shares the proxy’s bucket — see Troubleshooting. gRPC: RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED on unary RPCs from the per-tenant bucket; streaming RPCs are never rate limited. Disable via FS_RATE_LIMIT__ENABLED=false for load tests.
  • Checksum mismatch (hash_mismatch, 500 / DATA_LOSS) — The read path BLAKE3-verifies every chunk loaded from disk; a mismatch means on-disk corruption, not a client bug. Check file_storage_hash_mismatch_total in Monitoring, then follow the recovery steps in the Runbook (re-replicate or restore from backup; the affected blob must not be served).
  • Replication failed (replication_error, 502 / UNAVAILABLE) — The primary could not reach the target. Check replication.target_url and replication.api_key match the replica, network/DNS, and file_storage_replication_failures_total / replication_queue_depth. In sync mode the upload itself fails with 502; in async mode it is queued (backpressure at queue_capacity).
  • Validation errors (400 / INVALID_ARGUMENT) — the usual suspects: a file id that is not a 36-char UUID; a Range header not matching bytes=start-end|start-|-N (multi-range takes the first range); a hash that is not exactly 32 bytes / 64 hex chars on /internal/* endpoints; a multipart body without a file field; names/tags metadata fields over 4096 bytes.

Retry guidance: 429 and RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED (rate limit) are retryable after backoff; 500/DATA_LOSS are not; 502/UNAVAILABLE from replication are safe to retry once the target recovers. The Rust SDK retries unary calls on Unavailable/DeadlineExceeded/Aborted (3 attempts, 100 ms→2 s).

Configuration

Error behavior is not directly configurable, but several knobs change which errors occur: limits.* (413), rate_limit.* (429/RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED), replication.* (502/409), retention.* (whether purged files disappear). See the Configuration reference.

See also

  • REST — per-endpoint error lists
  • gRPC — per-RPC status codes
  • Authentication — 401 vs 403 semantics
  • Troubleshooting, Runbook
  • Monitoring — error metrics
  • Configuration reference

Authentication

file-storage authenticates users with username/password exchanged for an HS256 JWT access token plus an opaque, rotating refresh token. The same pair works on both protocols: REST takes the Authorization header, gRPC takes the authorization metadata. Roles (admin > user > viewer) and tenant membership are baked into the token and enforced on every request.

Overview

  • Access token — stateless HS256 JWT; TTL auth.access_ttl_secs (default 900 s = 15 min). Cannot be revoked; it simply expires.
  • Refresh token — opaque UUIDv7, TTL auth.refresh_ttl_secs (default 7 days), stored in memory server-side, single-use (rotated on every refresh), lost on restart.
  • Users come from config only (auth.users[]) — there is no user-registration endpoint. Passwords may be plaintext (password, dev) or an argon2id PHC string (password_hash, production).
  • Login attempts for unknown users burn a dummy argon2 verification, so response timing does not reveal whether the username exists.

How it works

POST /api/v1/auth/login verifies the password (argon2id against password_hash, or constant-time compare for password) and issues a pair. The JWT claims:

ClaimValue
subusername
tenanttenant UUID the user belongs to
roleadmin, user or viewer
iatissued-at (unix seconds)
expiat + access_ttl_secs
jtiUUIDv7 token id

Verification allows 5 s of clock leeway and pins the algorithm to HS256 with auth.jwt_secret — anyone holding that secret can mint valid tokens, so treat it like a root credential (Secrets).

Refresh rotation: POST /auth/refresh consumes the presented token and returns a new pair; the old refresh token is revoked immediately, so a replayed token fails with 401. Because the refresh store is in-memory, a restart logs every client out of refresh capability (access tokens keep working until they expire). Logout revokes only the presented refresh token and is idempotent; the access token remains usable until exp.

Usage

Login and use the token on both protocols:

curl -s localhost:8080/api/v1/auth/login \
  -d '{"username":"alice","password":"alice123"}'

{"access_token":"eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9.eyJzdWIiOiJhbGljZSIsInRlbmFudCI6IjAxOTMwMDAwLTAwMDAtNzAwMC04MDAwLTAwMDAwMDAwMDAwMiIsInJvbGUiOiJ1c2VyIiwiaWF0IjoxNzMwMDAwMDAwLCJleHAiOjE3MzAwMDA5MDAsImp0aSI6IjAxOTMwMDAwLW…","refresh_token":"01930000-0000-7000-8000-00000000feed","token_type":"Bearer","expires_in":900}

# REST
curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $ACCESS_TOKEN" localhost:8080/api/v1/files

# gRPC (metadata; lowercase key)
grpcurl -plaintext -H "authorization: Bearer $ACCESS_TOKEN" …

Role matrix (✓ allowed):

Operationadminuserviewer
Download / meta / list own tenant✓✓✓
Upload, soft delete (own tenant)✓✓—
Cross-tenant file access✓——
include_deleted listings✓——
Hard purge (?purge=true)✓——
Tenants create/list✓——
Audit query, GC trigger, resync✓——
Login/refresh/logout✓✓✓

Hashing passwords for production — generate an argon2id PHC string with the built-in subcommand and put it in auth.users[].password_hash (drop the password field):

file-storage hash-password --password 'S3cret-hunter2'
# $argon2id$v=19$m=65536,t=3,p=4$… (paste into config/auth.users[].password_hash)

Configuration

KeyDefaultNotes
auth.jwt_secret—≥ 16 chars, enforced at boot; rotate per Secrets
auth.access_ttl_secs900access-token lifetime
auth.refresh_ttl_secs604800refresh lifetime (7 d)
auth.users[].username / tenant_id / role—role ∈ admin|user|viewer; each user needs password or password_hash

The dev/default config ships the test users admin/admin123, alice/alice123, bob/bob123, viewer/viewer123 — never use them outside a sandbox.

Examples

Refresh (note the single-use rotation — store the new refresh token):

curl -s localhost:8080/api/v1/auth/refresh -d '{"refresh_token":"0193…feed"}'

Logout — revokes the refresh token, always 204:

curl -s -X POST localhost:8080/api/v1/auth/logout -d '{"refresh_token":"0193…feed"}' -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n'

A 401 body looks like {"error":{"code":"unauthorized","message":"invalid credentials"}}; a viewer attempting an upload gets 403 {"error":{"code":"forbidden","message":"write role required"}}.

  • Errors — 401/403 mapping
  • Configuration reference — auth.* keys
  • Secrets — jwt_secret rotation

Examples

Copy-paste recipes for the full file lifecycle — login, upload, download (full and ranged), metadata, listing, delete and GC — in curl, Rust (REST and gRPC SDK), Go and Python. All snippets target a local dev server on localhost:8080 (REST) and localhost:9090 (gRPC) with the default test users (alice/alice123), which exist only in the shipped dev config.

Overview

The same operations exist on both transports: REST is simplest for scripts and browsers, gRPC streams better for large payloads. Each snippet below is self-contained and stays within 20 lines; exact request/response schemas live in REST and gRPC. Errors follow the shared catalog in Errors, so a 404 on REST is NOT_FOUND on gRPC, and tokens obtained via one transport work on the other.

How it works

Auth once, then carry the token: REST wants Authorization: Bearer <jwt>; gRPC wants the authorization: Bearer <jwt> metadata (or x-api-key for the internal replication service). Uploads accept either multipart/form-data with a file field or a raw body with metadata in the query string; large payloads favor the raw-body form. Downloads honor ETag/If-None-Match (304) and Range on the /range route (inclusive end; multi-range takes the first range).

Usage

curl recipes (base URL http://localhost:8080, $FID = an uploaded file id):

# Login
TOKEN=$(curl -s localhost:8080/api/v1/auth/login \
  -d '{"username":"alice","password":"alice123"}' | jq -r .access_token)

# Upload, small (multipart)
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -F file=@notes.txt -F name=notes.txt -F tags=docs,draft \
  localhost:8080/api/v1/files

# Upload, large (raw body; stream-friendly)
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/octet-stream' \
  --data-binary @big.iso 'localhost:8080/api/v1/files?name=big.iso&tags=iso'

# Download full / range
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" localhost:8080/api/v1/files/$FID -o out.bin
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H 'Range: bytes=0-1023' \
  localhost:8080/api/v1/files/$FID/range -o head.bin

# Metadata (with chunk table) and listing
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" 'localhost:8080/api/v1/files/$FID/meta?chunks=true'
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" 'localhost:8080/api/v1/files?tag=docs&limit=10'

# Soft delete (user), hard purge (admin), GC trigger (admin)
curl -s -X DELETE -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" localhost:8080/api/v1/files/$FID
curl -s -X DELETE -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN" 'localhost:8080/api/v1/files/$FID?purge=true'
curl -s -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN" localhost:8080/api/v1/admin/gc

Examples

Rust (REST via reqwest)

use reqwest::multipart;

#[tokio::main]
async fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let c = reqwest::Client::new();
    let pair: serde_json::Value = c.post("http://localhost:8080/api/v1/auth/login")
        .json(&serde_json::json!({"username":"alice","password":"alice123"}))
        .send().await?.json().await?;
    let token = pair["access_token"].as_str().unwrap().to_string();

    let form = multipart::Form::new()
        .text("name", "notes.txt")
        .part("file", multipart::Part::reader(std::fs::File::open("notes.txt")?));
    let up: serde_json::Value = c.post("http://localhost:8080/api/v1/files")
        .bearer_auth(&token).multipart(form)
        .send().await?.json().await?;
    println!("uploaded {}", up["file_id"]);
    Ok(())
}

Rust (gRPC via the bundled SDK)

FileStorageClient handles token storage, 1 MiB upload pieces, gzip and unary retries; the server does the content-defined chunking.

use file_storage::grpc::FileStorageClient;

#[tokio::main]
async fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let mut c = FileStorageClient::connect("http://localhost:9090").await?;
    c.login("alice", "alice123").await?;

    let header = file_storage::grpc::UploadHeader {
        name: "notes.txt".into(), mime_type: "text/plain".into(), ..Default::default()
    };
    let up = c.upload(header, tokio::fs::File::open("notes.txt").await?).await?;
    let id = up.manifest.as_ref().unwrap().file_id.clone();

    let mut out = tokio::fs::File::create("copy.txt").await?;
    let hdr = c.download(&id, &mut out).await?; // verifies nothing lost: hdr.original_hash
    let mut sink = tokio::fs::File::create("head.bin").await?;
    c.download_range(&id, Some(0), Some(1024), &mut sink).await?;
    Ok(())
}

Go (grpc-go, generated types)

Generate types from proto/file_storage/v1 (go_package github.com/your-org/file-storage/api/v1;v1), then attach the bearer metadata manually — grpc-go has no built-in token layer here.

package main

import (
    "context"

    "google.golang.org/grpc"
    "google.golang.org/grpc/credentials/insecure"
    "google.golang.org/grpc/metadata"
    v1 "github.com/your-org/file-storage/api/v1"
)

func main() {
    conn, _ := grpc.NewClient("localhost:9090", // gRPC listener port
        grpc.WithTransportCredentials(insecure.NewCredentials()))
    defer conn.Close()

    login, _ := v1.NewAuthServiceClient(conn).Login(context.Background(),
        &v1.LoginRequest{Username: "alice", Password: "alice123"})
    ctx := metadata.AppendToOutgoingContext(context.Background(),
        "authorization", "Bearer "+login.AccessToken) // manual bearer metadata

    files := v1.NewFileServiceClient(conn)
    man, _ := files.GetManifest(ctx, &v1.GetManifestRequest{FileId: "<file-id>"})
    _, _ = files.DeleteFile(ctx, &v1.DeleteFileRequest{FileId: man.FileId})
}

Python (grpcio)

import grpc
import file_storage.v1.file_pb2 as fp
import file_storage.v1.file_pb2_grpc as fp_grpc
import file_storage.v1.auth_pb2 as fa
import file_storage.v1.auth_pb2_grpc as fa_grpc
import file_storage.v1.admin_pb2 as adm
import file_storage.v1.admin_pb2_grpc as adm_grpc

channel = grpc.insecure_channel("localhost:9090")
pair = fa_grpc.AuthServiceStub(channel).Login(
    fa.LoginRequest(username="alice", password="alice123"))
md = (("authorization", f"Bearer {pair.access_token}"),)

stub = fp_grpc.FileServiceStub(channel)
manifest = stub.GetManifest(
    fp.GetManifestRequest(file_id="<file-id>", include_chunks=True), metadata=md)
print(manifest.name, manifest.size)

Streaming upload from Python: build a generator that yields UploadChunk(payload=header) once, then UploadChunk(payload=data=…) pieces (~1 MiB) from the file, and call stub.Upload(gen, metadata=md) — the server chunks content itself. Admin operations follow the same pattern, e.g. GC with an admin login:

adm_grpc.AdminServiceStub(channel).TriggerGC(
    adm.TriggerGCRequest(mode=adm.TriggerGCRequest.REFCOUNT),
    metadata=admin_md)  # dry_run=True raises UNIMPLEMENTED

See also

  • REST and gRPC — exact request/response schemas
  • Authentication — token lifecycle details
  • Rust client, Go client, Python client — deeper SDK guides
  • CLI recipes — shell-first workflows

Configuration Reference

file-storage is configured from one YAML file (passed as the CLI argument, e.g. config/default.yaml) plus FS_-prefixed environment variables that override individual keys. This page lists every key with its type, default, constraints and the environment variable that overrides it. There is no hot reload: every change requires a process restart.

Overview

Configuration is a single AppConfig tree mirroring config/default.yaml. The full file is deserialized with serde(default), so unknown/new keys fall back to defaults and existing minimal YAML files keep working across upgrades. After loading (and after env overrides are merged) the config is validated; invalid values fail boot with a clear message rather than corrupting data.

How it works

Load order: YAML file first, then environment overrides (env wins — see Environment variables). Validation then enforces the cross-field rules below; anything invalid aborts startup before any listener binds. Paths in the config are relative to the process working directory unless absolute. Runtime behavior of several keys is observable via /metrics; see Monitoring.

Validation rules

Boot fails fast if any rule is violated:

  • auth.jwt_secret length ≥ 16 chars.
  • chunking: min > 0 and min ≤ avg ≤ max; max ≤ 16 MiB.
  • compression.level in 1..=22.
  • At least one auth.users entry, and every user needs password or password_hash.
  • replication.enabled=true requires replication.target_url and batch_size > 0.
  • storage.shard_depth ≤ 4.
  • server.grpc_tls.enabled=true requires non-empty cert_path and key_path.

Hot reload

None. Every key — including seemingly trivial ones like logging.level — is read once at startup; changing a value requires a restart. Plan production changes via rolling restarts (see Production).

Examples

Minimal working config (everything else defaults):

auth:
  jwt_secret: "at-least-16-characters-long"
  users:
    - username: admin
      password_hash: "$argon2id$v=19$m=65536,t=3,p=4$…"
      tenant_id: "01930000-0000-7000-8000-000000000001"
      role: admin
tenants:
  - id: "01930000-0000-7000-8000-000000000001"
    name: default

Equivalent one-off override without touching YAML:

FS_SERVER__PORT=8081 FS_STORAGE__SHARD_DEPTH=3 ./file-storage config/default.yaml

See also

  • Environment variables — FS_ override semantics and caveats
  • Production — walkthrough of config/production.yaml
  • Secrets — handling jwt_secret, api_key, key files
  • REST, TLS, Monitoring

Environment Variables

Every configuration key can be overridden from the environment without editing the YAML file, using the FS_ prefix and __ (double underscore) as the nesting separator. This is the idiomatic way to run the same image in different environments (Compose, Kubernetes, systemd) while keeping one config file. Environment values always win over YAML values.

Overview

The loader (config crate, Environment::with_prefix("FS").separator("__").try_parsing(true).ignore_empty(true)) maps variable names to config paths:

FS_SERVER__GRPC_PORT   →  server.grpc_port
FS_RATE_LIMIT__BURST   →  rate_limit.burst

Rules of the mapping:

  • Prefix FS_ is stripped; the remainder is split on __; each segment is lowercased to build the path; a single _ inside a segment is just part of the key name (FS_STORAGE__ROOT → storage.root).
  • try_parsing(true) means numbers and booleans are parsed into their real types: FS_RATE_LIMIT__PER_SECOND=1000 becomes an integer, FS_ENCRYPTION__ENABLED=true becomes a boolean, FS_STORAGE__SHARD_DEPTH=3 becomes a number. Everything else stays a string.
  • ignore_empty(true) means empty variables are ignored (FS_REPLICATION__TARGET_URL= leaves the YAML value in place) — use variable deletion to “unset”.
  • Env overrides apply on top of the YAML; a value defined only in the environment is added at that path.

How it works

Precedence: environment beats YAML beats serde defaults. Because the config struct is #[serde(default)], neither the YAML nor the environment needs to mention every key. The merged config is validated once at boot (see Configuration reference); an invalid env value (e.g. FS_COMPRESSION__LEVEL=99) aborts startup with the same validation error as the equivalent YAML mistake.

List-values caveat: sequence fields — tenants and auth.users — cannot be overridden via environment variables. The config crate maps environment variables to scalar leaves; there is no syntax to express a list of structs. Define lists in the YAML file only, and vary everything else with env vars. Attempting FS_TENANTS=... has no effect on the parsed config.

Configuration

Commonly used overrides (full key list in the Configuration reference):

VariablePathExampleEffect
FS_SERVER__PORTserver.port8081Move the REST listener
FS_SERVER__GRPC_PORTserver.grpc_port9091Move the gRPC listener
FS_SERVER__GRPC_TLS__ENABLEDserver.grpc_tls.enabledtrueServe gRPC over TLS
FS_SERVER__GRPC_TLS__CERT_PATHserver.grpc_tls.cert_path/tls/server.crtServer certificate
FS_STORAGE__ROOTstorage.root/var/lib/fs/blocksBlock-store location
FS_STORAGE__SHARD_DEPTHstorage.shard_depth2Directory fan-out (≤ 4)
FS_DATABASE__PATHdatabase.path/var/lib/fs/index.redbredb index file
FS_CHUNKING__MAXchunking.max131072Max chunk bytes (≤ 16 MiB)
FS_COMPRESSION__LEVELcompression.level3zstd level 1..=22
FS_AUTH__JWT_SECRETauth.jwt_secret<64 hex>HS256 secret (≥ 16 chars)
FS_ENCRYPTION__ENABLEDencryption.enabledtrueAES-256-GCM at rest
FS_RATE_LIMIT__PER_SECONDrate_limit.per_second1000Rate-limit refill
FS_REPLICATION__ENABLEDreplication.enabledtrueWAL shipping on
FS_REPLICATION__TARGET_URLreplication.target_urlhttp://replica:8080Replica base URL
FS_REPLICATION__MODEreplication.modeasyncasync or sync
FS_RETENTION__GC_MODEretention.gc_moderefcountGC strategy
FS_LIMITS__MAX_FILE_SIZElimits.max_file_size10737418240Per-upload cap
FS_LOGGING__FORMATlogging.formatjsonpretty or json
FS_LOGGING__LEVELlogging.levelinfoLog verbosity

Examples

Docker Compose — override just what differs per environment; the image’s YAML stays untouched:

services:
  file-storage:
    image: file-storage:latest
    command: ["/app/file-storage", "/etc/file-storage/config.yaml"]
    environment:
      FS_SERVER__PORT: "8080"
      FS_SERVER__GRPC_PORT: "9090"
      FS_AUTH__JWT_SECRET: "${JWT_SECRET}"          # from .env / secret store
      FS_RATE_LIMIT__PER_SECOND: "1000"
      FS_REPLICATION__ENABLED: "true"
      FS_REPLICATION__TARGET_URL: "http://replica:8080"
      FS_LIMITS__MAX_FILE_SIZE: "10737418240"
      FS_LOGGING__FORMAT: "json"
    ports: ["8080:8080", "9090:9090"]

Equivalent shell invocation for quick experiments:

FS_SERVER__GRPC_TLS__ENABLED=true \
FS_SERVER__GRPC_TLS__CERT_PATH=/tls/server.crt \
FS_SERVER__GRPC_TLS__KEY_PATH=/tls/server.key \
FS_LOGGING__LEVEL=debug ./file-storage config/default.yaml

Sanity-check which values actually landed:

curl -s localhost:8080/health   # version/uptime — then check logs for the loaded config

Remember that all env-based changes still require a restart to take effect (no hot reload), and that secrets in environment variables are readable from /proc/<pid>/environ — see Secrets for the trade-offs.
